  //    Validates exactly one root joint, parent-before-child ordering,
  //    and updates matrixWorld BEFORE calculateInverses() (otherwise
  //    every inverse is identity and vertices stretch from the origin
  //    when SkinnedMesh applies the bind pose — see upstream comment).
  function buildSkeleton(graph: RigGraph): SkeletonBuildResult;

  // 2. Build smooth skin weights (4-influence per vertex, nearest joints
  //    by squared-distance falloff). Returns a Uint16BufferAttribute for
  //    skinIndex and a Float32BufferAttribute for skinWeight, plus
  //    maxWeightError (the worst |1 - sum| across the geometry — the
  //    AI should surface this in its pre-emission checklist).
  function buildSemanticWeights(
    geometry: THREE.BufferGeometry,
    skeleton: SkeletonBuildResult,
    joints: RigJoint[],
    maxInfluences?: number,        // default 4
  ): WeightBuildResult;

  // 3. Build COMPACT skin weights (uint8 indices + uint8 normalized
  //    weights, 1 influence per vertex, weight = 255). For
  //    reconstructed multipart surfaces where smoothing across an
  //    unknown source seam would be less truthful than a rigid semantic
  //    region. Throws if the skeleton has > 255 bones (uint8 index cap).
  function buildRigidSemanticWeights(
    geometry: THREE.BufferGeometry,
    skeleton: SkeletonBuildResult,
    resolveJoint: (x: number, y: number, z: number, vertex: number) => string,
  ): CompactWeightBuildResult;

  // 4. Validate a RigGraph BEFORE compiling it. Returns string[] of
  //    human-readable errors (empty array = clean). Checks for:
  //    duplicate joint ids, self-parenting, NaN / Infinity rest
  //    positions, missing parent joints, and root count != 1.
  function validateRigGraph(graph: RigGraph): string[];

  // 5. Validate a geometry's skinIndex + skinWeight attributes. Returns
  //    string[] of human-readable errors. Checks for: skinWeight vec4
  //    presence, skinIndex vec4 presence, and per-vertex |sum - 1|
  //    within the supplied tolerance (default 0.001). An empty return
  //    value is the 6th item in the pre-emission checklist below.
  function validateWeights(geometry: THREE.BufferGeometry, tolerance?: number): string[];

  // Bonus, not part of the "5 public" but documented for completeness
  // (also exported by the bundle, free, no extra cost):
  //   resetSkeleton(result):       re-pose a built skeleton to bind
  //   jointWorldPosition(result, id): query a joint's world position
  //   asVector([x,y,z]):           convert a Vec3 literal to THREE.Vector3

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
34.3 — THE 3 DATA SHAPES (re-declared for spec self-containment)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
The bundle ships the source-of-truth types INLINE — no external type
imports. The spec must be self-contained, so the canonical shapes are
re-declared here verbatim. If the upstream character-ir.ts source is
attached in a future round, the types in this section are what to expect.

  // [number, number, number] — XYZ literal. THREE.Vector3-compatible.
  type Vec3 = [number, number, number];

  // [x, y, z, w] — quaternion in XYZW order. The bundle's
  // bone.quaternion.set(...restRotation) consumes this order directly.
  type Quat4 = [number, number, number, number];

  // One joint in the rig graph. parentId is OMITTED for the root.
  // restPosition is WORLD-space (the build pass subtracts the parent's
  // rest position to derive a local offset — see buildSkeleton above).
  // restRotation is WORLD-space quaternion in XYZW order. Bone NAMES
  // are the same .name strings, so they must follow PART 32 error [2]'s
  // alphanumeric-with-underscores rule and (for any humanoid / biped /
  // mech) reuse STANDARD_BONES from PART 33.1[10].
  interface RigJoint {
    id: string;                    // unique, alphanumeric + underscore
    parentId?: string;             // OMITTED on the single root
    restPosition: Vec3;            // [x, y, z] world-space
    restRotation: Quat4;           // [x, y, z, w] world-space, unit length
  }

  // The full rig graph. joints[] does NOT need to be topologically
  // sorted — buildSkeleton re-orders via a pending-queue walk that
  // defers a joint until its parent has been added. But sorting it
  // parent-before-child is fine and helps the AI's own debugging.
  interface RigGraph {
    joints: RigJoint[];
  }

The bundle ALSO exports two return-type shapes (free, not part of the
"3 data shapes" but useful for editor / .d.ts consumers):

  interface SkeletonBuildResult {
    root: THREE.Bone;
    bones: Map<string, THREE.Bone>;
    skeleton: THREE.Skeleton;
    restWorldPositions: Map<string, THREE.Vector3>;
  }
  interface WeightBuildResult {
    indices: THREE.Uint16BufferAttribute;
    weights: THREE.Float32BufferAttribute;
    maxWeightError: number;
  }
  interface CompactWeightBuildResult {
    indices: THREE.Uint8BufferAttribute;
    weights: THREE.Uint8BufferAttribute;
  }

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
34.4 — WHEN TO USE RIG HELPERS VS. THE PLAIN PIVOT HIERARCHY (PART 30)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 30 (pivot hierarchy + auto-promotion + single-bone-per-part rigid
skin at export) is still the DEFAULT. PART 34 is the explicit escape
hatch for the cases PART 30 cannot cover. Use the decision table below:

  ┌────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┬───────────────────┐
  │ If the model needs...          │ Use PART 30          │ Use PART 34       │
  │                                │ (pivot hierarchy,    │ (three-rig-helpers│
  │                                │  default)            │  escape hatch)    │
  ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ Mesh deforms with bones        │ NO (mesh is rigidly │ YES               │
  │ (vertex skin weights)          │ bound, 1 bone/part)  │                   │
  ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ Joint rotates but mesh doesn't │ YES (default)        │ NO (overkill)     │
  │ deform (low-poly, static pose) │                      │                   │
  ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ Smooth skin (4-influence per   │ NO                   │ YES               │
  │ vertex, distance falloff)      │                      │ (buildSemantic    │
  │                                │                      │  Weights)         │
  ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ Multi-bone-per-vertex (> 1     │ NO                   │ YES               │
  │ influence per vertex)          │                      │                   │
  ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ > 255 bones in the rig graph   │ NO                   │ YES (but only     │
  │                                │                      │ with the float    │
  │                                │                      │ path —            │
  │                                │                      │ buildRigid-       │
  │                                │                      │ SemanticWeights   │
  │                                │                      │ caps at 255)      │
  ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ Simple human / biped / mech    │ YES (use STANDARD_   │ NO                │
  │ (< 64 bones, rigid per-part)   │ BONES, no helpers)   │                   │
  ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ Style-locked cel-toon /        │ YES                  │ NO                │
  │ retro-psx / hologram mode      │                      │                   │
  │ (PART 33.1[4])                 │                      │                   │
  ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────────────┤
  │ External GLB-viewer rig-test   │ YES (PART 30.7's     │ YES (already      │
  │ mode wants a `skins` section   │ auto-promotion       │  has a real       │
  │                                │  covers it)          │  SkinnedMesh —    │
  │                                │                      │  export is a      │
  │                                │                      │  no-op identity   │
  │                                │                      │  in this case)    │
  └────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────┴───────────────────┘

Quick rule of thumb: if the model has fewer than 64 bones, no vertex
skin weights, and each part is a rigid mesh that follows its parent
Group's transform, you DO NOT need this PART — use PART 30 and forget
34 exists. Reach for 34 only when the table above says YES.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
34.5 — WORKED EXAMPLE: SMOOTH-SKINNED ROBOT (validate → build skeleton
        → build mesh → validate weights → expose userData.rigGraph)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
The end-to-end pattern for a model that genuinely needs vertex skin
weights. Adapted from the maintainer's own robot-rigging build session
to fit this renderer's single-file factory contract. Drop this skeleton
into a real createXxxModel() factory — DO NOT paste it as a standalone
file with no factory wrapper (PART 30.0's load-time rejection applies).

  import * as THREE from 'three';
  import {
    buildSkeleton,
    buildSemanticWeights,
    validateRigGraph,
    validateWeights,
  } from 'three-rig-helpers';

  export function createSmoothSkinnedRobotModel(options?: { color?: number }): THREE.Group {
    const root = new THREE.Group();
    root.name = 'RobotRoot';

    // 1. Declare the rig graph (world-space rest positions, parent links).
    //    Note: parentId omitted on the single root; restRotation is a unit
    //    quaternion (0,0,0,1) for a default-pose robot.
    const rig: RigGraph = {
      joints: [
        { id: 'Bone_Pelvis', restPosition: [0, 1.0, 0], restRotation: [0, 0, 0, 1] },
        { id: 'Bone_Spine',  parentId: 'Bone_Pelvis', restPosition: [0, 1.25, 0], restRotation: [0, 0, 0, 1] },
        { id: 'Bone_Chest',  parentId: 'Bone_Spine',  restPosition: [0, 1.55, 0], restRotation: [0, 0, 0, 1] },
        { id: 'Bone_Head',   parentId: 'Bone_Chest',  restPosition: [0, 1.85, 0], restRotation: [0, 0, 0, 1] },
        { id: 'Bone_Shoulder_L', parentId: 'Bone_Chest', restPosition: [ 0.25, 1.65, 0], restRotation: [0, 0, 0, 1] },
        { id: 'Bone_Arm_L',      parentId: 'Bone_Shoulder_L', restPosition: [ 0.50, 1.55, 0], restRotation: [0, 0, 0, 1] },
        { id: 'Bone_Shoulder_R', parentId: 'Bone_Chest', restPosition: [-0.25, 1.65, 0], restRotation: [0, 0, 0, 1] },
        { id: 'Bone_Arm_R',      parentId: 'Bone_Shoulder_R', restPosition: [-0.50, 1.55, 0], restRotation: [0, 0, 0, 1] },
      ],
    };

    // 2. Validate BEFORE building — fail fast, surface every error.
    const rigErrors = validateRigGraph(rig);
    if (rigErrors.length > 0) {
      throw new Error('rig graph validation failed:\n  - ' + rigErrors.join('\n  - '));
    }

    // 3. Compile the rig → THREE.Skeleton + bone map.
    const built = buildSkeleton(rig);

    // 4. Build the geometry (procedural torso + head, no PART 30 auto-
    //    promotion needed because we're authoring the SkinnedMesh
    //    ourselves; the auto-promotion path is a no-op for true
    //    SkinnedMeshes).
    const torsoGeo = new THREE.CylinderGeometry(0.20, 0.22, 0.55, 8, 4, true);
    torsoGeo.translate(0, 0.275, 0);   // base at y=0 (parent local)

    // 5. Build smooth skin weights (4-influence, distance falloff).
    const w = buildSemanticWeights(torsoGeo, built, rig.joints);
    if (w.maxWeightError > 0.01) {
      console.warn('[rig] torso weights max error =', w.maxWeightError,
                  '(> 0.01 — geometry may show seams at joint boundaries)');
    }

    // 6. Validate the weights BEFORE assembling the SkinnedMesh.
    const weightErrors = validateWeights(torsoGeo);
    if (weightErrors.length > 0) {
      throw new Error('skin weight validation failed:\n  - ' + weightErrors.join('\n  - '));
    }

    // 7. Assemble the SkinnedMesh and wire it to the root bone.
    const torsoMat = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({
      color: options?.color ?? 0x8aa0b8,
      roughness: 0.55, metalness: 0.35,
    });
    const torsoMesh = new THREE.SkinnedMesh(torsoGeo, torsoMat);
    built.root.add(torsoMesh);
    // CRITICAL: bind the SkinnedMesh to the compiled skeleton explicitly.
    // (Auto-bind via mesh.skeleton = built.skeleton is the conventional
    //  shape; the renderer's GLB-export path picks it up either way.)
    torsoMesh.bind(built.skeleton, built.root.matrixWorld);

    // 8. Expose the runtime contract on userData so the renderer can
    //    surface a "Rig" button + the joint hierarchy panel.
    root.userData.rigGraph = rig;                                    // PART 34 contract
    root.userData.skeletonSource = 'three-rig-helpers';              // PART 34 marker
    root.userData.skeleton = built.skeleton;                         // optional: live THREE.Skeleton
    root.userData.bones = built.bones;                               // optional: id → THREE.Bone map
    root.userData.restWorldPositions = built.restWorldPositions;     // optional: id → world pos

    return root;
  }

That is the whole pattern: validate → build skeleton → build geometry →
build weights → validate weights → assemble SkinnedMesh → expose
userData.rigGraph. Every other line in the file is normal PART 1-33
factory body code (materials, head sphere, optional PART 33.1[5]
animationFsm, etc.).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
34.6 — PRE-EMISSION CHECKLIST (the 6 items — MANDATORY for any model
        that imports 'three-rig-helpers'; a no-op for every other
        model that does not)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Append these to PART 32.4's 16-item structural reference checklist.
Any unchecked item is a load-time or first-render failure that the
renderer will surface as a red spec chip.

  [ ] 1. [PART 34.1] The file contains exactly ONE `import ... from
         'three-rig-helpers'` line, at the top, and zero other local /
         relative / npm / fetch imports. PART 4.1 / 4.2 still apply to
         everything else in the file.
  [ ] 2. [PART 34.3] The rig graph has been validated with
         `validateRigGraph()` and the returned string[] is empty.
  [ ] 3. [PART 34.3] The graph declares exactly one root joint (the
         one with parentId omitted). buildSkeleton throws on != 1, but
         validate first so the error message is yours, not its.
  [ ] 4. [PART 32 error [2]] Every joint's `id` is alphanumeric +
         underscore, non-empty, and (for any humanoid / biped / mech)
         matches the STANDARD_BONES catalog from PART 33.1[10]. No
         hard-coded alternates like `Bone_Thigh_Left` or
         `bone-thigh-l` — the case and the underscore matter because
         external rig-test viewers and the renderer's Rig panel both
         match by exact string.
  [ ] 5. [PART 34.5] The geometry's `skinIndex` + `skinWeight` have
         been validated with `validateWeights()` and the returned
         string[] is empty (the 6th item in this checklist, surfaced
         loudly so the AI doesn't ship a model with seams that
         stretch from the origin).
  [ ] 6. [PART 34.5] The returned root Group has
         `root.userData.rigGraph` (the RigGraph itself) AND
         `root.userData.skeletonSource === 'three-rig-helpers'` set.
         The renderer uses these two keys to enable the Rig button +
         run the E11 validator check at load time.

If a model does not import 'three-rig-helpers' at all, this checklist
is a no-op — don't add fake entries to satisfy the form. Models that
don't need it (i.e. most low-poly cases) skip 34 entirely.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
34.7 — RUNTIME CONTRACT (what the renderer does when it sees
        userData.rigGraph)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
The renderer's `window.lblSpec.refreshOnLoad()` (in index.html, the
RJS UPDATE 3 block) now performs three rig-aware behaviors on every
load — these are observable by any model that exports PART 34's
contract, and inert for every other model:

  (a) VALIDATOR E11 (rig helpers marker check)
      If `root.userData.rigGraph` is present but
      `root.userData.skeletonSource !== 'three-rig-helpers'`, the
      renderer surfaces a non-blocking warning via the spec chip —
      "Rig graph declared but skeletonSource is missing or not
      'three-rig-helpers'". This is a STRICT check: the only
      sanctioned source is the pre-bundled helpers. A model with a
      custom-built SkinnedMesh is allowed (PART 30.7's auto-promotion
      + per-part skin handles it) but should NOT also declare
      userData.rigGraph — pick one path or the other.

  (b) RIG BUTTON (topbar, next to the Sockets button)
      If `root.userData.rigGraph` is present AND
      `root.userData.skeletonSource === 'three-rig-helpers'`, the
      renderer enables a new Rig button in the topbar (right after
      the Sockets button, before Recipes). Clicking it opens a panel
      listing every joint: id, parent id, and the rest position
      (read from the rigGraph on every open). The panel re-validates
      the graph with `validateRigGraph()` on every open and surfaces
      any errors as a red banner above the joint list. The validator
      runs CLIENT-SIDE — it does not require the bundle to be
      imported dynamically; the bundle is loaded once at page load via
      the import map and the helpers are available globally.

  (c) GLB EXPORT OPTIMIZATION (optional, buildSkinnedHierarchyForExport)
      The existing `buildSkinnedHierarchyForExport` (index.html line
      ~14069, see PART 30.7) is the renderer's auto Bone-promotion +
      per-part skin pass. If the loaded model ALREADY has a real
      SkinnedMesh with skinIndex + skinWeight from PART 34, the
      export pass detects that and is a no-op for those meshes (no
      need to re-bind what the factory already bound correctly). The
      optimization is internal — the export button is unchanged, no
      new button, no new workflow.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
34.8 — CROSS-REFERENCES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 34 layers on top of, never replaces:

  - PART 4.1 / 4.2 — the single-file / no-relative-import rule. PART 34
    is the one explicit exception; the rest of PART 4 still applies.
  - PART 30 (rigging & animation authoring standard) — the default
    pivot-hierarchy approach is still the right answer for ~99% of
    cases. Reach for 34 only when 30's auto-promotion + single-bone-
    per-part rigid skin is insufficient.
  - PART 30.2 — bone naming rule. Every joint id must be alphanumeric
    + underscore and (for humanoids / bipeds / mechs) reuse
    STANDARD_BONES from PART 33.1[10].
  - PART 30.7 — the auto-promotion + per-part skin pass is the
    renderer's existing skeleton export path. PART 34 is a strictly
    more powerful alternative for the 1% of cases that need it.
  - PART 32 error [2] — track binding. Every animated bone still needs
    an explicit, alphanumeric .name. The Rig panel's joint list reads
    the same .name strings.
  - PART 32.4 (16-item structural reference checklist) — the 6 items
    in 34.6 are an APPEND to that list, not a replacement.
  - PART 33.1[10] / 33.5 — the universal rigging standard + the
    STANDARD_BONES catalog. Reuse the canonical names; don't invent
    alternates.
  - PART 33.4 (10-item architectural checklist) — orthogonal. None of
    33.4's items change. A model that imports 'three-rig-helpers'
    still benefits from any of 33.1[1-9] that apply to the subject.

PART 34 does NOT introduce:
  - Any new field that PART 30 / 33 doesn't already accept
  - Any new renderer behavior beyond (a) the E11 validator, (b) the Rig
    button, and (c) the export-path no-op described in 34.7
  - Any new dependency in the model file beyond the single
    'three-rig-helpers' import
  - Any change to the GLB export's user-visible behavior

================================================================================
END OF PART 34 — v1.11 ADDITIONS: PRE-BUNDLED RIG HELPERS (OPT-IN)
The escape hatch is opt-in and narrow: use PART 30 (pivot hierarchy +
auto-promotion) for every model that doesn't need true SkinnedMesh
vertex skin weights; reach for PART 34 only when the decision table in
34.4 says YES. The 6-item checklist in 34.6 is MANDATORY for any model
that imports 'three-rig-helpers' (no-op for every other model). Nothing
in this PART removes or weakens any rule from PARTs 1-33.
================================================================================

================================================================================
PART 35 — v1.12 / v8.4 ADDITIONS: TRIANGLE-FIRST GEOMETRY (TRIS)
The "everything is a triangle" mental model. Source: the attached
WORKING_WITH_TRIANGLES guide. Layers on top of PART 1 (single-file),
PART 2 (types), PART 32 (canonical helpers — already shipped an
`inspectModelTriangles` stub; PART 35 EXTENDS that stub with a
per-geometry breakdown), PART 4 (no relative imports), PART 30
(rigging), and PART 34 (rig helpers). The renderer gains a new
`🔺 Tris` topbar button + E12 validator check that surfaces the
triangle/vertex/draw-call counts of the currently-loaded model.
================================================================================

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
35.1 — EXECUTIVE OVERVIEW: HOW TRIS WORK IN GPU RASTERIZATION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
In WebGL, WebGPU, OpenGL, and DirectX, the fundamental unit of every
3D surface is a TRIANGLE (a "tri"). Every Three.js geometry
(`BoxGeometry`, `CylinderGeometry`, `SphereGeometry`, ...) is just a
collection of triangles stored inside a `THREE.BufferGeometry`.

A single triangle requires:
  - 3 VERTICES — points in 3D space [X, Y, Z].
  - 1 NORMAL   — surface-facing direction [NX, NY, NZ], for lighting.
  - 3 UVs      — 2D texture-mapping coordinates [U, V].

When building procedural models, you can move from stacking primitive
shapes to working DIRECTLY with triangles via two distinct workflows:

  1. COMPOSE & MERGE — build parts from transformed primitive shapes,
     then merge every part's triangles into ONE `THREE.BufferGeometry`
     via `mergeGeometries`. Result: 1 draw call per bone instead of 10.
  2. RAW PROCEDURAL TRIS — define exact vertex coords + triangle face
     indices directly via `Float32Array` + `Uint16Array`. Result: full
     control over topology (armor plates, sci-fi cockpits, mechanical
     claws, etc.).

PART 35 documents five methods, all compatible with the LBL spec's
single-file / no-relative-imports / no-`three/addons/`-outside-the-
allow-list rules: every helper below is reachable from a .ts factory
through the renderer-side `window.lblSpec` re-exports OR through a
verbatim copy of the function (the spec is self-contained).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
35.2 — METHOD 1: MERGING COMPOSED SHAPES INTO UNIFIED TRIANGLE BUFFERS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Instead of adding 10 separate mini-meshes to a bone (10 GPU draw
calls), combine every underlying triangle into ONE BufferGeometry via
`mergeGeometries`.

Benefits:
  - Performance: reduces 100+ draw calls down to 5-10.
  - Single transform, single material slot per merged part.
  - Clean GLB / glTF / JSON export.

TypeScript reference implementation (the canonical helper every .ts
factory may copy verbatim, or reach for via
`window.lblSpec.helpers.createUnifiedLimbSegment` — see 35.9):

  import * as THREE from 'three';
  import { mergeGeometries }
    from 'three/addons/utils/BufferGeometryUtils.js';

  export function createUnifiedLimbSegment(
    bone: THREE.Object3D,
    material: THREE.Material
  ): THREE.Mesh {
    // 1. Instantiate individual geometric components.
    const mainBeam     = new THREE.BoxGeometry(0.18, 0.22, 1.20);
    const topPlate     = new THREE.BoxGeometry(0.20, 0.05, 1.15);
    const pistonCyl    = new THREE.CylinderGeometry(0.04, 0.04, 0.80, 8);
    const kneeJointCap = new THREE.CylinderGeometry(0.12, 0.12, 0.22, 12);

    // 2. Position / rotate each component relative to the joint origin.
    topPlate.applyMatrix4(
      new THREE.Matrix4().makeTranslation(0, 0.12, 0.60));

    const rotPiston = new THREE.Matrix4().makeRotationX(Math.PI / 2);
    const posPiston = new THREE.Matrix4().makeTranslation(0, -0.08, 0.50);
    pistonCyl.applyMatrix4(posPiston.multiply(rotPiston));

    const rotCap = new THREE.Matrix4().makeRotationZ(Math.PI / 2);
    const posCap = new THREE.Matrix4().makeTranslation(0, 0, 1.20);
    kneeJointCap.applyMatrix4(posCap.multiply(rotCap));

    mainBeam.applyMatrix4(
      new THREE.Matrix4().makeTranslation(0, 0, 0.60));

    // 3. Force non-indexed for uniform vertex structures.
    const shapesToMerge = [
      mainBeam.toNonIndexed(),
      topPlate.toNonIndexed(),
      pistonCyl.toNonIndexed(),
      kneeJointCap.toNonIndexed()
    ];
    shapesToMerge.forEach(g => {
      if (!g.attributes.normal) g.computeVertexNormals();
      if (!g.attributes.uv) {
        g.setAttribute('uv', new THREE.BufferAttribute(
          new Float32Array(g.attributes.position.count * 2), 2));
      }
    });

    // 4. Merge into a single unified triangle BufferGeometry.
    const merged = mergeGeometries(shapesToMerge, false);
    merged.computeVertexNormals();

    // 5. Wrap in a single Mesh and parent it to the bone.
    const mesh = new THREE.Mesh(merged, material);
    mesh.castShadow = true;
    mesh.receiveShadow = true;
    bone.add(mesh);
    return mesh;
  }

RULE: prefer mergeGeometries whenever a single body part has 2+
sub-meshes. The renderer's PART 30.7 auto Bone-promotion pass detects
that a bone has multiple children and emits a soft warning (see E12
below). Merging fixes that warning AND cuts the draw-call count.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
35.3 — METHOD 2: CONSTRUCTING CUSTOM GEOMETRY TRIANGLE-BY-TRIANGLE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Use this for armor plates, blades, sci-fi cockpits, mechanical claws,
or any shape whose topology you want exact control over.

----- 35.3.A — Non-Indexed Triangles (3 vertices per triangle) -----

Each triangle is 3 sequential points in the position array. Triangle
0 = vertices [0,1,2]; triangle 1 = [3,4,5]; etc.

  import * as THREE from 'three';

  export function createLowPolyKneeBlade(): THREE.BufferGeometry {
    const geom = new THREE.BufferGeometry();

    const vertices = new Float32Array([
      // Triangle 1: Upper Right Face
       0.0,  0.75,  0.0,   // Apex tip
       0.18, 0.0,   0.08,  // Mid right
       0.0,  0.0,   0.25,  // Mid center ridge

      // Triangle 2: Upper Left Face
       0.0,  0.75,  0.0,
       0.0,  0.0,   0.25,
      -0.18, 0.0,   0.08,

      // Triangle 3: Lower Right Bevel
       0.0,  0.0,   0.25,
       0.18, 0.0,   0.08,
       0.0, -0.45,  0.05,

      // Triangle 4: Lower Left Bevel
       0.0,  0.0,   0.25,
       0.0, -0.45,  0.05,
      -0.18, 0.0,   0.08,

      // Triangle 5: Back Base Top
       0.0,  0.75,  0.0,
      -0.18, 0.0,   0.08,
       0.18, 0.0,   0.08,

      // Triangle 6: Back Base Bottom
      -0.18, 0.0,   0.08,
       0.0, -0.45,  0.05,
       0.18, 0.0,   0.08
    ]);

    geom.setAttribute('position',
      new THREE.BufferAttribute(vertices, 3));
    geom.computeVertexNormals();
    return geom;
  }

----- 35.3.B — Indexed Triangles (shared vertices + index array) -----

Indexed geometry stores unique vertices ONCE and defines triangles
via an index array (`Uint16Array`). Use this when the same vertex
position is shared by multiple triangles (5 verts → 6 triangles in
the example below vs 18 verts for the same shape non-indexed).

  export function createIndexedPyramid(): THREE.BufferGeometry {
    const geom = new THREE.BufferGeometry();

    const positions = new Float32Array([
       0.0,  0.5,  0.0,  // 0: Top Tip
      -0.5,  0.0, -0.5,  // 1: Back Left
       0.5,  0.0, -0.5,  // 2: Back Right
       0.5,  0.0,  0.5,  // 3: Front Right
      -0.5,  0.0,  0.5   // 4: Front Left
    ]);

    const indices = new Uint16Array([
      // 4 side triangles
      0, 4, 3,   // Front
      0, 3, 2,   // Right
      0, 2, 1,   // Back
      0, 1, 4,   // Left
      // 2 base triangles
      1, 2, 3,
      1, 3, 4
    ]);

    geom.setAttribute('position',
      new THREE.BufferAttribute(positions, 3));
    geom.setIndex(new THREE.BufferAttribute(indices, 1));
    geom.computeVertexNormals();
    return geom;
  }

DECISION TABLE (35.3.A vs 35.3.B):
  - 35.3.A (non-indexed): use when each triangle is its own
    independent face (decals, splinters, floating armor panels,
    anything with hard edges + flatShading: true).
  - 35.3.B (indexed):     use when vertices are shared (spheres,
    cylinders, organic curves, any surface that smooth-shades).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
35.4 — METHOD 3: LOW-POLY FLAT SHADING & SEGMENT OPTIMIZATION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------

----- 35.4.A — Flat Shading Material -----

To render distinct, faceted low-poly planes with crisp edge
highlights, enable `flatShading: true` on the material:

  const lowPolyMechMaterial = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({
    color:       0xe65c00,   // Industrial Orange
    metalness:   0.25,
    roughness:   0.40,
    flatShading: true         // sharp, visible triangle facets
  });

----- 35.4.B — Controlling Triangle Density on Primitives -----

When using procedural Three.js shapes, adjust radial + height
segments to target specific triangle budgets:

  Low-Poly Barrel / Piston:
    new THREE.CylinderGeometry(0.05, 0.05, 1.2, 6)
    -> 6 radial segments = 12 side tris + 8 cap tris = 20 tris.

  Low-Poly Cockpit / Torso Sphere:
    new THREE.SphereGeometry(0.6, 8, 6)
    -> 8 × 6 = 48 tris.

  Low-Poly Quadruped Foot Talon / Spike:
    new THREE.ConeGeometry(0.12, 0.35, 4)
    -> 4 radial segments = 4 side tris + 2 base tris = 6 tris.

  Low-Poly Turret Base:
    new THREE.CylinderGeometry(0.6, 0.7, 0.25, 8)
    -> 8 radial = 16 side + 12 cap (top) + 12 cap (bottom) = 40 tris.

The renderer's E12 validator (35.9) reads each model's `totalTriangles`
and surfaces a soft warning if it exceeds 50,000 (rule of thumb for
60 FPS at 1080p on a mid-range laptop with this scene complexity).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
35.5 — METHOD 4: TRIANGLE COUNT CALCULATOR & REAL-TIME MESH INSPECTOR
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
This is the EXTENSION of PART 32.2's `inspectModelTriangles` stub.
PART 32 shipped a small helper that returned { triangles, meshes,
materials, bones, clips }. PART 35 extends it with a richer
per-geometry breakdown that the 🔺 Tris panel uses.

  export interface MeshTriangleMetrics {
    // Original PART 32 fields (unchanged for back-compat):
    triangles: number;
    meshes:    number;
    materials: number;
    bones:     number;
    clips:     number;

    // NEW in PART 35 (renderer-side inspector reads these):
    totalTriangles: number;
    totalVertices:  number;
    drawCalls:      number;        // = mesh count for non-instanced
    geometries: Array<{
      name:      string;
      triangles: number;
      vertices:  number;
      indexed:   boolean;
    }>;
  }

  export function inspectModelTriangles(
    root: THREE.Object3D
  ): MeshTriangleMetrics {
    let totalTriangles = 0, totalVertices = 0, meshCount = 0;
    const materials   = new Set<THREE.Material>();
    const bones       = new Set<THREE.Bone>();
    const geometries: MeshTriangleMetrics['geometries'] = [];

    root.traverse(child => {
      const m = child as THREE.Mesh;
      if (!m.isMesh) return;
      meshCount++;
      const g = m.geometry; if (!g) return;

      let tris = 0, verts = 0;
      if (g.index) tris = g.index.count / 3;
      else if (g.attributes.position) tris = g.attributes.position.count / 3;
      if (g.attributes.position) verts = g.attributes.position.count;

      totalTriangles += tris;
      totalVertices  += verts;

      const mat = m.material as
        THREE.Material | THREE.Material[] | undefined;
      if (Array.isArray(mat)) mat.forEach(x => materials.add(x));
      else if (mat) materials.add(mat);

      geometries.push({
        name:      m.name || `Mesh_${meshCount}`,
        triangles: Math.floor(tris),
        vertices:  verts,
        indexed:   !!g.index
      });
    });
    root.traverse(o => {
      if ((o as THREE.Bone).isBone) bones.add(o as THREE.Bone);
    });
    const clips: THREE.AnimationClip[] = (root as any).animations || [];
    return {
      // PART 32 (back-compat):
      triangles: Math.round(totalTriangles),
      meshes:    meshCount,
      materials: materials.size,
      bones:     bones.size,
      clips:     clips.length,
      // PART 35 (extended):
      totalTriangles: Math.floor(totalTriangles),
      totalVertices:  totalVertices,
      drawCalls:      meshCount,
      geometries
    };
  }

The function is available TWO ways from any .ts factory:

  1. Copy the verbatim reference above into your file.
  2. Import from the renderer's re-export at runtime:
       const m = (window as any).lblSpec.helpers
                   .inspectModelTriangles(rootGroup);

Option 2 is the recommended path: the renderer's copy is the same
function the 🔺 Tris panel uses, so the AI factory's output and the
inspector's display never disagree.

JSON/JS path: the renderer's `window.lblSpec.helpers` exposes
`inspectModelTriangles` directly to blueprint evaluators. Blueprints
that need metrics on a per-frame basis (e.g. an LOD auto-switcher)
read `window.lblSpec.lastTriMetrics` after every `buildScene()`.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
35.6 — METHOD 5: RIGGING & GLB / JSON EXPORT WITH TRIANGLE MESHES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
When using merged or custom triangle meshes with an animated bone
armature:

  1. BONE PARENTING. Attach each merged triangle mesh DIRECTLY as a
     child of its respective `THREE.Bone` (or `THREE.Group` for the
     pivot-hierarchy default from PART 30):
       thighBone.add(mergedThighTriangleMesh);
       kneeBone.add(mergedShinTriangleMesh);

  2. WORLD MATRIX UPDATE. Always call `rootGroup.updateMatrixWorld(true)`
     after building your triangle meshes. Without this, the merged
     mesh's `matrixWorld` lags by one frame, and the rig panel's
     "rest position" readout in PART 34 will show stale coordinates.

  3. GLTF / GLB / JSON EXPORT. When exported via `GLTFExporter` (or
     the renderer's JSON/JS blueprint serializer), all triangle
     indices, vertex attributes (position + normal + uv), and parent
     node transforms are baked into the output container. The
     `inspectModelTriangles` count is the LAST line of the renderer's
     export toast, so the user can confirm what shipped.

PART 35 does NOT introduce a new export path. It documents the
correct ORDER of operations for the EXISTING path: build tris →
parent to bone → update world matrix → export.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
35.7 — COMPLETE COPY-PASTEABLE IMPLEMENTATION MODULE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
A drop-in factory that builds a low-poly turret using ALL of Methods
1-3 from above. Useful as a starting point for mech / vehicle / sci-
fi props. Ships with both the merged-triangle path (default) and the
raw-triangle blade path (commented out at the bottom).

  import * as THREE from 'three';
  import { mergeGeometries }
    from 'three/addons/utils/BufferGeometryUtils.js';

  /**
   * Build a low-poly turret using merged triangle geometry.
   * Returns a single Mesh ready to add to a bone / group.
   */
  export function buildLowPolyTurretWithTris(
    material: THREE.Material
  ): THREE.Mesh {
    // 1. Primitive shapes with controlled low-poly segments.
    const baseGeom = new THREE.CylinderGeometry(0.6, 0.7, 0.25, 8);
    const domeGeom = new THREE.SphereGeometry(0.45, 8, 6);
    const gunGeom  = new THREE.BoxGeometry(0.12, 0.14, 0.90);

    // 2. Position sub-components.
    domeGeom.applyMatrix4(
      new THREE.Matrix4().makeTranslation(0, 0.20, 0));
    gunGeom.applyMatrix4(
      new THREE.Matrix4().makeTranslation(0, 0.22, 0.45));

    // 3. Merge into a unified triangle buffer (1 draw call).
    const merged = mergeGeometries([
      baseGeom.toNonIndexed(),
      domeGeom.toNonIndexed(),
      gunGeom.toNonIndexed()
    ], false);
    merged.computeVertexNormals();

    const turretMesh = new THREE.Mesh(merged, material);
    turretMesh.name = 'LowPolyTurret_TriangleMesh';
    turretMesh.castShadow    = true;
    turretMesh.receiveShadow = true;
    return turretMesh;
  }

This function is exposed on `window.lblSpec.helpers` (35.9.b below)
so any .ts factory can call it WITHOUT copying the body.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
35.8 — PRE-EMISSION CHECKLIST (append to PART 32.3's 16-item list)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Any unchecked item is a soft warning (E12 below) surfaced via the
🔺 Tris button.

  [ ] 17. [PART 35.2] Every body part with 2+ child meshes is merged
         via `mergeGeometries` into a single BufferGeometry before
         being added to a bone. (The renderer's E12 surfaces a
         warning otherwise.)
  [ ] 18. [PART 35.3] Custom geometry either uses 35.3.A (non-
         indexed) with a fresh Float32Array per triangle, OR 35.3.B
         (indexed) with a Uint16Array index buffer. Mixed approaches
         in the same model are an anti-pattern.
  [ ] 19. [PART 35.4] Low-poly materials declare `flatShading: true`
         when the intent is faceted; smooth-shaded organic models
         leave it off and use indexed geometry from 35.3.B.
  [ ] 20. [PART 35.4] Total triangle budget stays under 50,000 for
         a single model (60 FPS on mid-range hardware). The renderer's
         E12 reads the inspectModelTriangles count and warns above
         this threshold.
  [ ] 21. [PART 35.6] After building all triangle meshes,
         `rootGroup.updateMatrixWorld(true)` is called once before
         any export or animation playback.
  [ ] 22. [PART 35.5] The factory either (a) copies the canonical
         `inspectModelTriangles` from 35.5 verbatim OR (b) calls
         `window.lblSpec.helpers.inspectModelTriangles(rootGroup)`
         to get the same metrics the 🔺 Tris panel uses.

If a model uses none of PART 35's methods (purely primitive geometry,
no merging, no raw tris), this checklist is a no-op — don't add fake
entries to satisfy the form.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
35.9 — RUNTIME CONTRACT (what the renderer does)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
The renderer's `window.lblSpec` object (RJS UPDATE 3 / RJS UPDATE 4
block in index.html) gains three PART 35-specific behaviors on every
load — these are observable by any model that uses PART 35's
helpers, and INERT for every other model.

  (a) VALIDATOR E12 (triangle density + un-merged-mesh check)
      Runs on every `window.lblSpec.refreshOnLoad()` call. Reads
      `inspectModelTriangles(currentRoot)`. Surfaces a non-blocking
      warning via the spec chip when EITHER of the following is
      true:
        - `totalTriangles > 50_000` ("Model uses N triangles
          (PART 35 soft cap: 50,000). Consider merging or
          decimating.")
        - A single Bone / Group has >= 3 child meshes
          ("Bone '<name>' has N child meshes (PART 35.2 prefers
          mergeGeometries into a single BufferGeometry per part).")
      Multiple warnings stack on the chip.

  (b) 🔺 TRIS BUTTON (topbar, between "Rig" and "Recipes")
      Enabled for EVERY loaded model — there is no
      `userData.rigGraph` / `userData.skeletonSource` gate here, the
      metrics exist for every model that has at least one Mesh.
      Clicking it opens a panel showing:
        - Total triangles, total vertices, mesh count (= draw calls)
        - Per-geometry breakdown table (name, triangles, vertices,
          indexed?) — sorted by triangle count descending
        - A "Copy JSON" button that puts the full metrics object on
          the clipboard (so the AI can paste it back into a prompt
          when iterating on a model)
        - A "Re-measure" button that re-runs inspectModelTriangles
          on the current scene (useful after live edits)
      The button is ALWAYS enabled (never `disabled`). Models with
      zero meshes (an empty scene) get a "No meshes to inspect"
      placeholder.

  (c) lblSpec.helpers RE-EXPORTS
      The renderer exposes the following PART 35 functions on
      `window.lblSpec.helpers` so .ts factories can use them
      without copying the body:
        - inspectModelTriangles(root): MeshTriangleMetrics
        - createUnifiedLimbSegment(bone, mat): THREE.Mesh
        - createLowPolyKneeBlade(): THREE.BufferGeometry
        - createIndexedPyramid():    THREE.BufferGeometry
        - buildLowPolyTurretWithTris(material): THREE.Mesh
      These are the SAME functions the spec defines (35.5, 35.2,
      35.3.A, 35.3.B, 35.7). A factory that calls them gets the
      same behavior the 🔺 Tris panel measures.

35.10 — CROSS-REFERENCES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 35 layers on top of, never replaces:

  - PART 1 (single-file rule). 35.2/35.3/35.7 use only `'three'` and
    `'three/addons/utils/BufferGeometryUtils.js'` (allowed by PART 4
    / PART 34.1's allow-list).
  - PART 2 (typed public surface). The `MeshTriangleMetrics`
    extension in 35.5 keeps the original 5 fields from PART 32.2
    and adds 4 new ones — a back-compat additive change.
  - PART 4 (no relative imports). 35.2/35.3/35.7 only import from
    the allowed `'three'` and `'three/addons/'` specifiers.
  - PART 30 (rigging & animation). 35.6 documents the correct
    bone-parenting order for merged triangle meshes; reinforces —
    never replaces — PART 30.7's auto Bone-promotion.
  - PART 32 (canonical helpers). 35.5 EXTENDS PART 32.2's
    `inspectModelTriangles` with a per-geometry breakdown; the
    original 5-field shape is preserved.
  - PART 32.3 (16-item checklist). 35.8's 6 items APPEND to that
    list (17-22), not a replacement.
  - PART 33 (architectural features). 35.4.B's 50,000-tri soft cap
    is consistent with PART 33.1's existing complexity budgets.
  - PART 34 (rig helpers). 35.6's "updateMatrixWorld(true) before
    export" is the same fix that PART 34.7(a) requires for the
    Rig panel to read correct rest positions.
  - PART 34.6 (6-item checklist). Orthogonal — none of PART 35's
    items overlap with PART 34's items.

PART 35 does NOT introduce:
  - Any new relative import (still PART 4.1 / 4.2 clean)
  - Any new dependency in the model file (helpers reachable via
    `window.lblSpec.helpers.*` instead)
  - Any new GLB-export user-visible behavior (35.6 documents the
    EXISTING path, doesn't add a new path)
  - Any removal or weakening of rules from PARTs 1-34
